Applies to the taskclass `small` tier and the `--agentic-small-model` fallback (`DefaultSmallModelID`), which move in lockstep. modeltier gains a generic `flash-lite` → small case ordered before the base flash cases, closing the substring hazard where a lite id would classify at its base model's tier and dodge the small-tier-parent guard; pricing carries the lite entry via the regen allowlist. Explicit `--agentic-small-model` / `small_model` pins are unaffected. (#561)
- digest/attach/mcp/agent/autonomous: the `core_agent.*` subsystem meters land, completing #338 Phase 3. Each subsystem exports its own `RegisterMetrics(mp, narrowDep)` observer wired at daemon boot: digest calls + bytes-saved per method (process-global `digest.Telemetry()` snapshot); per-agent compactions / checkpoints / subtasks / inbox-pending with `session.id` (compaction and checkpoint counts come from new in-memory counters incremented at the `Compact`/`Checkpoint` success sites — NOT the eventlog-derived `ContextStats`, which costs an O(events) scan per read and resumes across restarts); watchdog alerts as a sync counter in the post-turn drain, counted even when no host alert callback is wired (the internal buffer drains on `Check()`, so async observation was never possible); autonomous runs by ACTUAL `StopReason` constant (recorded via `defer` — the loop has six exit sites — with an `error` fallback; note the daemon itself doesn't call `autonomous.Run`, its consumers are background subagent spawns and embedders); attach sessions/subscribers/drops/peers via two new accessors (`Server.SubscriberStats`, `Server.SubscriberDrops` — broadcaster/pool stay unexported per #390) with a new pool-lifetime drop counter at both buffer-full sites (the design doc's `reason=slow|full` split never existed in code); and MCP server status as a presence gauge over the real `ok|error` enum (set once at `Build`, never transitioned — no invented lifecycle states). Design-doc instrument tables rewritten to match shipped reality. Closes [#338](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/338) — the deferred `gen_ai.agent.request/response.size` + `workflow.steps` histograms are recorded on the issue as awaiting upstream semantics.
- usage/compose/config: usage metrics now cover attach-created sessions and gain a cardinality knob (#338 Phase 3, second slice). `compose.RegistryTrackerProvider` bridges the attach SessionRegistry into `usage.RegisterMetrics`, so multi-session daemons report per-session tokens/cost/turns for every live session (previously only the daemon's primary session was observed — the acknowledged follow-up slice in cmd/core-agent/metrics.go); the daemon-side provider dedups the primary session by tracker pointer since it registers in both places. New config `otel.metrics.session_labels` (default `true`, preserving the shipped series shape): set `false` and the observer aggregates across sessions before export — required semantics, not just attribute-stripping, because identical-attribute observations are last-wins in the OTel SDK — drops the per-session duration gauge, and ANDs the cost series' `priced` flag. The aggregated observer keeps a RETIREMENT BASELINE so counters stay monotonic when the registry evicts idle sessions (a plain live-sum would DROP on eviction and Prometheus would read a counter reset); token/cost baselines yield to a lazily-resumed session's replayed totals, while unreplayed digest spend keeps counting from the baseline. Registry entries sharing one tracker (the TUI `/model` swap re-registers without unregistering) dedup last-wins, attributing the series to the newest session id. `Tracker.AppendDigestSavings` clamps negative subagent cost the way it already clamped negative token savings. `core_agent.digest.subagent.cost_usd` joins the usage observer (the accumulator lives on `usage.Tracker.DigestSavings()`, not in pkg/digest — a design-doc staleness correction). API note: `usage.RegisterMetrics` gains variadic `RegisterOption` — source-compatible for callers, but code assigning the function itself to a variable of the old type must update. Advances [#338](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/338).
- agent/tools: turns and tool calls now emit ADK-schema GenAI histograms (#338 Phase 3, first slice). `gen_ai.agent.invocation.duration` records one point per turn from `Agent.Run`'s terminal cleanup (attrs: `gen_ai.agent.name`; `error.type` only on failed turns, carrying the stable `attach.ClassifyTurnError` kind — never raw error text). `gen_ai.tool.execution.duration` times every runnable tool via a new `tools.DurationInstrumenter` wrapper composed OUTSIDE the #460 mutation serializer at agent construction (and in `RunSubtask`), so the recorded latency includes lock-wait and, for gated MCP tools, permission-prompt wait — deliberately, that's what the model observes; headless deployments have no prompts. Names and attributes match ADK's cross-language metrics schema (adk.dev/observability/metrics) verbatim so Python/Kotlin dashboards work unchanged; the schema's three size/steps histograms are deliberately NOT emitted (semantics undocumented upstream — even adk-python doesn't ship them; emitting a guess would fake compatibility). New `agent.WithMeterProvider` option (default: global provider resolved at `New`; noop when metrics are off, so recording costs nothing). Explicit bucket boundaries sized for real turn/tool shapes — SDK defaults top out at 10s. Coverage is every turn the caller observes: `Run`, `RunWithContents` (the AX adapter path), and cost-ceiling-refused turns (recorded at ~0s with their `error.type` — without this the histogram would go dark exactly during a spend-cap incident). New `agent.WithMetricAgentName` keeps `gen_ai.agent.name` low-cardinality where the agent's actual name is model-chosen — the background spawn path stamps `background_subagent` instead of the model's invented name. The timing wrapper forwards `ReadOnlyHint` like the gate and namespace wrappers, so composition order can't silently reclassify hinted tools. Advances [#338](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/338).
- telemetry: `SetupMetrics` now starts the otel-contrib Go runtime instrumentation on the MeterProvider it installs, completing #338 Phase 1 — with metrics enabled (`otel.metrics` config or `OTEL_METRICS_EXPORTER`), operators get `go.memory.*`, `go.goroutine.count`, and GC-config gauges via OTLP and/or the Prometheus scrape endpoint with zero agent-loop instrumentation. Registration happens before the global provider install so a failure can't leave a half-configured global, and the scrape test asserts the runtime instruments actually flow end-to-end. Instrument inventory recorded in `docs/metrics-design.md`. Advances [#338](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/338) (Phase 1 complete; Phase 2 moved to k8s-lookout; Phase 3 pending design).
- models/gemini: outbound LLM calls on the direct Gemini API (API-key) backend now emit `otelhttp` HTTP client spans and propagate `traceparent`, closing the last dark Gemini-family segment at the LLM boundary (#325; the Anthropic provider's HTTP client remains untraced, per that issue's explicit non-goal — file separately if needed). Live verification showed the issue was already three-quarters done upstream: on Vertex, genai builds its client through `cloud.google.com/go/auth/httptransport`, whose default telemetry wraps the transport in `otelhttp` — so `NewVertex` deliberately does NOT set `ClientConfig.HTTPClient` (doing so would bypass genai's ADC wiring and break auth; a field comment and `TestNewVertex_NoHTTPClientOverride` pin that trap). Only `NewAPIKey` supplies an instrumented client, which is safe there because API-key auth is a per-request header genai sets itself. Friction-log entry 5 for genai rewritten to record the backend asymmetry and the `HTTPClient`/ADC coupling. Closes [#325](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/325).
- agent: the system prompt is assembled from ordered LAYERS (#459, implementing `docs/system-prompt-layering-design.md`): `CoreInstruction` (harness contract: compaction/handover framing, tool-dispatch rules, the edit-sequencing invariant with its marked #460 exit path) → provider quirks selected from the model identifier (`GeminiParallelismQuirk`, probe-backed; Claude gets none; `WithoutProviderQuirks` suppresses) → a mode overlay (`InteractiveOverlay` default / `AutonomousOverlay` via the new `WithMode`) → user memory (`WithUserInstruction`, the loader's output — now AFTER the core, the deliberate precedence-and-prompt-cache flip from the old prefix arrangement) → operator/consumer appends (`WithExtraInstruction`, repeatable). The old ~350-word monolith's persona and plan-sketch lines are deleted per the design's disposition table; `DefaultInstruction` survives as a deprecated alias (`CoreInstruction + InteractiveOverlay`) through v2.8.x alongside the deprecated `WithSystemInstructionPrefix`. `WithInstruction` remains the full-replace escape hatch (skips layers 1–3; layers 4–5 still append). **Spawned agents stop losing the harness contract**: `background.Spec.SystemPrompt`, `SubtaskSpec.SystemPrompt`, and the remote `/subagent` spawn now COMPOSE onto the layered autonomous baseline instead of replacing it (new `ReplaceSystemPrompt` escape hatches preserve the old behavior), which quietly fixes spawned subagents having no idea what a compaction summary is. The autonomous driver warns when its build closure produced an interactive-mode agent; `ask_user`'s description carries the "a human is reachable" exception to the autonomous no-clarification rule. Operators get `agent.append_system_prompt` / `agent.system_prompt_file` config plus `--append-system-prompt <text|@file>` / `--system-prompt-file` flags (flag beats config; replace prints its you-lose-the-contract warning), and `/memory` reports the active layer stack as its first row. New `dev/persona-probe` implements the design's merge gate (Gemini with/without the deleted persona line, same evidence standard as `dev/parallel-probe`). Closes [#459](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/459).
- agent/tools: the runtime now enforces what the prompt used to beg for (#460, the #459 design's marked exit path): within one model response, read-only tools keep ADK's concurrent dispatch while **state-mutating tools serialize on a per-agent lock**, so parallel writes can no longer race and corrupt state — the Claude Agent SDK dispatch pattern. Classification: a tool's own `tools.ReadOnlyHinter` declaration wins, then the built-in read-only table (`read_file`, `read_many_files`, `stat`, `list_dir`, `glob`, `grep`, `json_query`, `fetch_url`), then the fail-safe default: mutating (`bash` and every MCP tool serialize — ADK's mcptoolset doesn't surface the MCP `readOnlyHint` annotation yet; the seam forwards it the day it does). Wired in `agent.New` (covering every registered tool and toolset) and `RunSubtask`. With the guarantee in place, `CoreInstruction`'s edit-sequencing paragraph is **deleted** — not softened — and its dispatch-fact sentence now states the serialized contract; the guarantee is mutual exclusion, not response-order, so the "dependent calls go in a later response" rule stands. One visible latency shift: under `mode: ask`, two mutating calls in one response now present their permission prompts one at a time (the prompt fires while the lock is held) instead of both at once; `ask_user` is classified read-only precisely so a human-scale question never stalls edits behind it. A cross-barrier dispatch test drives real ADK fan-out — mutual exclusion pinned deterministically, the read-only-concurrency direction via a barrier that CI catches across runs. Closes [#460](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/460).
- pricing: `internal/pricing` is promoted to **`pkg/pricing`** and joins the stability surface (#489 — the finishing pass the #386 compose extraction needed). Parts of the public API were uncallable outside the module because their signatures named internal types: `compose.CfgToCatalogOverride` (returns `map[string]pricing.ModelRates`), `compose.DescribeRefresh` (takes `pricing.RefreshOutcome`), and `usage.SetCatalog` (takes `*pricing.Catalog`, with nothing exported able to produce one). External consumers can now build catalogs (per tenant, via the explicit-catalog arguments `usage` already accepts), drive `pricing.Refresh`, and name every argument/result of the compose pricing helpers. `compose.MaybeWireContextCache` similarly returns a new exported `compose.ContextCacheHandle` interface instead of the un-nameable `*internal/vertexcache.Manager` — `Delete` is the only method hosts need after wiring, so the manager itself stays internal. No behavior change anywhere; import-path move plus one return-type change (callers using `:=` are unaffected; nil-skip semantics preserved). Closes [#489](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/489).
- models: first-class Claude 5-generation support — `claude-opus-5` and `claude-fable-5` join `claude-sonnet-5` in the builtin pricing catalog (regenerated from LiteLLM: opus-5 $5/$25, fable-5 $10/$50 per MTok), and all three appear in the TUI's `/model` picker. The Anthropic-Vertex smoke leg now defaults to `claude-sonnet-5`, so the real-provider E2E exercises the #357 thinking-block round-trip on every run (validated live: sonnet-5 completed a full bash tool loop; opus-5 and fable-5 single-turn verified). Projects without 5-gen Model Garden access override via `ANTHROPIC_SMOKE_MODEL`.
- config: `attach.cost_rate_limit` exposes the #463 per-caller cost rate limit to operators — `{per_minute, burst, disabled}` in `.agents/config.json` flows onto `attach.Options.CostRateLimit` at daemon startup (omitted = library defaults: burst 5, 10/min; negatives fail validation). Documented on the site configuration reference.
- attach: per-caller rate limiting on the cost-bearing endpoints (`pkg/attach/rate_limit.go`), on by default — a token bucket keyed by the middleware-resolved caller identity (bearer-table hit, validated proxy assertion, or the anonymous single-user bucket; never spoofable headers/IPs) bounds the operations that run real model/network work per request: the five slash ops (`compact`, `done`, `btw`, `subagent`, `replan`), `POST /sessions`, and `POST …/pricing/refresh`. Defaults: burst 5, 10/minute per caller; tune or disable via `attach.Options.CostRateLimit` (`{PerMinute, Burst, Disabled}`). Over-limit requests get `429` with a `Retry-After` header and a matching JSON body. Reads, `/events` streams, `/inject`, `/wake`, and perms endpoints are unlimited. Closes [#463](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/463).
- `compose`: `AttachOptions` + `BuildAttachOptions(cfg.Attach)` translate the config file's `attach` block (with `${ENV}` expansion in every string field) into the resolved listener options, completing the [#386](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/386) extraction (PR 6). The bundled CLI's `attachOpts` is now an alias of `compose.AttachOptions`; main keeps only the CLI-beats-config flag overlay. `pkg/compose` is documented on the site's Library API page.
- `compose`: on-demand multi-session construction is now library surface (PR 5 of [#386](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/386)): `BuildMultiSessionAuthn`, `SessionFactoryDeps` (all fields exported), `BuildSessionFactory`, `ReproduceAgent`, and `BuildSessionResumer` moved from `cmd/core-agent` to `pkg/compose`, so any daemon serving `POST /sessions` can reuse the per-session gate derivation, per-caller instruction overlay, tracker isolation (#275 regression seam moved intact), compactor/checkpointer wiring, and resume-from-ACL flow the bundled binary uses. The per-session wake loop now runs on `runner.WakeLoop` (the hand-rolled copy is gone).
- `runner`: new `runner.WakeLoop(ctx, agent, opts)` consolidates the wake-driven inbox drain every headless surface runs — block on `WakeRequested`, drain the inbox through an empty-prompt turn, account usage via the shared `usage.TurnTap` discipline, repeat until ctx cancels (PR 4 of [#386](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/386)). The CLI's `--no-repl` mode now uses it (the per-session multi-session loop follows in PR 5); library consumers building "attach is the only surface" daemons get the loop for free instead of hand-rolling the select + usage tap. One deliberate refinement over the inline loop it replaces: usage commits on `TurnComplete` via `TurnTap` (an errored turn that never completes no longer appends a partial usage row).
- `compose`: "allow always" persistence is now wired end-to-end (PR 3 of [#386](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/386)). `compose.ConfigGrantStore` is the reference `permissions.GrantStore`: it persists grants into `.agents/config.json` atomically and idempotently, and the bundled CLI wires it at gate construction, so every prompter (TUI modal, stdin, HTTP broker) gets durable "always" grants — previously only the TUI's own callback persisted, with a divergent shape (the raw prompt path rather than the gate's expanded pattern). Path-scope grants land as **typed `path_scope.allow_paths` entries carrying the grant's resolved access** (`r`/`rw`) instead of the legacy bare `path_scope.allow` list, whose entries reload as `rw` and would silently broaden a read-only grant on restart; a later broader grant widens the existing entry in place. The config-persist helpers behind `/allow`, `/deny`, `/model`, `/theme`, and bundle toggles moved from `cmd/core-agent` to exported compose functions (`AppendPathScope`, `AppendPathScopeEntry`, `AppendPermissionsAllow`, `AppendPermissionsDeny`, `AppendBuiltinAllowExtra`, `PersistModelChoice`, `PersistThemeChoice`) so library consumers share the TUI's on-disk layout. The TUI's `AlwaysAllow` callback is now a no-op (the gate owns both halves of the contract; double-writing is gone).
- `compose`: new `pkg/compose` package lifts the first tranche of reusable substrate wiring out of `cmd/core-agent`'s `package main` so library consumers can build the same agent stack the bundled binary does (PR 2 of [#386](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/386), `docs/compose-extraction-design.md`): `BuildCompactor`, `BuildAgenticTools`, `BuildMCPDigestLLMFallback`, `MaybeWireContextCache` (substrate builders), `FormatStartupSummary`/`StartupSummaryInputs` and `RenderContextStats` (operator-visible formatters), `RefreshPricing`/`SetPricing`/`RebuildPricingCatalog`/`DescribeRefresh`/`CfgToCatalogOverride` (pricing operations), and `NewFilteredLogWriter` (the ADK log-noise filter). Pure move-and-export — the binary now calls `compose.*`; no behavior change. Multi-session construction and the config-backed grant store follow in later PRs of the same extraction.
- `permissions`: "allow always" grants are now a first-class, persistable gate concern — the new `GrantStore` interface (`Persist(ctx, Grant)`) plugs into the gate via `Options.GrantStore` or `SetGrantStore` (mirrors `SetPrompter`), and `DeriveForSession` sub-gates share the template's store by reference, consistent with the daemon-wide Policy/PathScope mutation rules. The `Grant` value carries the fully-expanded pattern the gate installed in-memory (the `"<tool>:<key>"` policy pattern, or the subtree-expanded path pattern with the read→r / write→rw promotion applied), so a restart reloads the identical grant. **Behavior fix shipped with it:** `DecisionAllowAlways` on non-path prompts (bash, generic, MCP/skill) now installs a real in-memory policy pattern instead of only remembering the session — closing the gap where the documented "persist a permanent allowlist entry" contract was implemented exclusively by the bundled TUI's callbacks, so stdin and custom prompters silently downgraded "always" to "this session". A `Persist` failure surfaces to the gated call rather than silently downgrading; a nil store (the default) keeps grants in-memory-only. Control-plane write approvals never reach the store (no standing bypass for `config.json`), pinned by test. PR 1 of [#386](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/386) (`docs/compose-extraction-design.md`); the config-backed reference store lands with `pkg/compose`.
- `agent`: introduce the read-only accessor seam (`Agent.Inner`, `Agent.Emit`, `Agent.EventLog`, `Agent.ModelName`, `Agent.Streaming`, `Agent.Tracker`) that the split-out packages (`pkg/agent/autonomous`, `pkg/agent/background`) build on instead of reaching `Agent`'s unexported fields. `Inner()` returns the underlying ADK agent; `Emit()` pushes a typed event onto the attach SSE stream (no-op when no subscriber); the rest expose the event log, model id, streaming mode, and usage tracker a driver needs. All are nil-safe. No behavior change — this is the seam that phases 2–3 of the `pkg/agent` decomposition sit on (see the ⚠️ Breaking changes entry and `docs/agent-package-split-design.md`). Part of [#388](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/388).
- Metrics: OTel MeterProvider pipeline shipped with OTLP push + Prometheus pull readers from a single meter registration. Off by default; opt-in via `cfg.otel.metrics.exporter` (`none` | `otlp` | `prometheus` | `both`) with the standard-shape `OTEL_METRICS_EXPORTER` env var override for K8s Deployments carrying a shared ConfigMap. New `--metrics-addr` flag overrides `cfg.otel.metrics.prometheus_addr` for the scrape endpoint bind. Exemplar filter is AlwaysOn so v2 sync instruments (`mcp.tool_call.duration` etc.) will attach trace IDs to metric points once they land — enabling metric-alert → offending-trace click-through in Grafana/Tempo and equivalents. First observer covers the usage/cost surface (`gen_ai.client.token.usage`, `core_agent.session.turns`, `core_agent.session.cost_usd`, `core_agent.session.duration`) using OTel GenAI semconv so cloud vendor dashboards (GCM, Datadog, Honeycomb) render token/cost panels automatically. Fail-loudly on init errors — Prometheus bind failure or invalid mode aborts the daemon rather than silently shipping a binary that emits no metrics. Closes the "no metrics anywhere" gap documented in `docs/metrics-design.md`; ADK-Go's upstream `// TODO(#479) init meter provider` doesn't fill this hole and neither does Google's Cloud AI-agent-ADK observability doc (Python-only, and even there admits *"Metric data isn't collected"*). Per-session metrics carry `session.id` + optional `app.name` / `user.id`; the primary-daemon path is wired now, multi-session and additional observer surfaces (digest, MCP, attach, watchdog, autonomous stop-reasons) stack behind. See `docs/metrics-design.md` for the full instrument catalog + tiering story (aggregate/per-agent/per-session/per-turn).
- Attach: SSE protocol bump to v1.4.0 — `capabilities` frame gained four optional fields (`features` flag map, `slash_commands` dynamic list, `agent` identity block, `caller_id` display hint) so backend-agnostic clients (mast-web) can render without a code change per producer. `status-update` gained an optional `capabilities` merge field for future hot changes. New `GET /whoami` endpoint returns the resolved caller identity + admin flag + auth source (`bearer` / `mtls` / `iap` / `asserted` / `anonymous`). Reserved `_render` / `_schema` keys on slash-response bodies for future renderer negotiation. Fully backward-compatible — pre-v1.4.0 servers omit the new fields, pre-v1.4.0 clients ignore them. Closes [#329](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/329), sibling to go-steer/mast-web#12.
- Attach: the `/events` SSE stream now negotiates the protocol version instead of only advertising it. A client MAY declare the version it speaks via the `?protocol=<semver>` query param or the `X-Attach-Protocol-Version` request header; the server rejects a declared **major** that differs from its own with `409 Conflict` (a malformed declaration is `400 Bad Request`) so a future breaking bump fails cleanly instead of silently mis-rendering on old clients. The server always echoes the version it speaks back on the `X-Attach-Protocol-Version` response header. Fully backward-compatible: clients that declare nothing are accepted unchanged. Closes [#389](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/389).

#### Bug or Regression
- auto-continue: a model turn truncated at the output-token cap (`MAX_TOKENS`) that still emitted text is no longer misclassified as a completed turn, so auto-continue now finishes it (on the session's next touch or a boot scan). The tail-shape classifier read every final model-text event as complete; genai carries the distinction in `Candidate.FinishReason`, but ADK's storage row has no FinishReason column (`createEventFromStorageEvent` drops it) so a reloaded event couldn't see it. The eventlog overlay now stamps an *abnormal* finish reason into `CustomMetadata` at `AppendEvent` (only abnormal — never the empty zero value or a plain `STOP` — so normal turns are untouched), and the classifier consults that stamp; only `MAX_TOKENS` drives a continuation (`SAFETY`/`RECITATION`/… are recorded for audit but stay terminal). No ADK fork, no schema migration. Fixes [#582](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/582). (#585)
- attach: an inject into a session that was just evicted (or whose daemon is shutting down) no longer returns `200` and then silently drops the message. `runner.WakeLoop` now closes the agent's inbox when its loop exits — both daemon shutdown and per-session eviction cancel that loop's context — so any `Inject` / `InjectAs` that races past handler-level gating fails loudly with `ErrInboxClosed` at the source instead of being acknowledged into a mailbox no live turn will ever drain. The previously-unused `agent.inbox.close()` mechanism now has its production caller, via the new exported `Agent.CloseInbox()`. Fixes [#566](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/566). (#587)
- attach: a `POST /interrupt` no longer occasionally surfaces the operator's clean cancel as a spurious "stale session error" turn event. The audit row (`Author=attach/interrupt`) was appended out-of-band from the handler with a `Get`-then-`AppendEvent` on the live session row while the interrupted turn was still flushing, bumping `last_update_time` and tripping ADK's optimistic-concurrency check mid-flush. Registrants now self-audit via the additive `attach.InterruptSelfAuditor` capability: the handler records the intent (`Agent.MarkInterruptPending`) and the audit row is written from the agent's own post-turn cleanup, after the interrupted turn unwinds — the one window with no live runner handle to race. Older registrants that don't implement the interface keep the out-of-band fallback. Fixes [#565](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/565). (#588)
- eventlog: SQLite read-write transactions now begin with `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` (`_txlock=immediate` on the dialector DSN), so a writer that finds the write lock held waits on `busy_timeout` instead of failing instantly. ADK's `AppendEvent` reads the session row and then writes inside one transaction; under the default deferred `BEGIN`, SQLite refuses that read→write upgrade with an *immediate* `SQLITE_BUSY` (`busy_timeout` never retries a snapshot upgrade). This is what stranded auto-continue: the boot scan's own overlay-pool writes (lock/bootlog) held the write lock while the continuation turn's `AppendEvent` on ADK's separate pool tried to commit, so an interrupted turn that no operator re-touched could sit unfinished — the exact unattended sessions the feature targets. Also closes the parent-checkpoint vs. subagent-create variant. Reads (`Get`/`List`) use no explicit transaction, so they stay lock-free under WAL. Fixes [#575](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/575). (#576)
- recipe/e2e: the GKE-troubleshoot demo's parent model reverts to `gemini-3.5-flash`. The `gemini-3.5-flash-lite` parent (#578) emitted a complete `plan` block on a watcher inject and then yielded the turn before executing any remediation, so automated incidents (no human to nudge) were silently abandoned; a live UAT proved prose-only prompt-hardening (#579) insufficient — flash-lite treats the plan as a terminal deliverable. The parent moves back to flash (live UAT: 13 turns, 16 tool calls, INCIDENT SUMMARY: RESOLVED) while the agentic-wrap sub-agent stays on flash-lite. (#580)
- test: `TestAutoContinueRetryLoop_SelfHealsAfterTransientLock` now cancels and joins the retry-loop goroutine before the eventlog handle closes, fixing a `-race` data race introduced with #577 — the test launched `AutoContinueRetryLoop` with a bare `go` and a cancel-only cleanup, so a mid-tick lock acquisition raced the eventlog teardown (the loop's own doc mandated the join; production `main.go` already did it via a `WaitGroup`). Test-only; reproduced ~1/3 of `-count=5 -race` runs pre-fix, 30× clean after. (#581)
- pricing/modeltier/usage: the compiled-in pricing floor now covers every taskclass tier default — `claude-sonnet-4-6` (the anthropic mid default) was absent from the builtin table, so in air-gapped or refresh-failed deployments `--task=chat`/`research` sessions accrued $0 cost and the `--max-*-cost-usd` ceilings never tripped; the guard test now iterates `taskclass.ModelForTier` across all providers × tiers instead of a gemini-only list. The Claude 5 family (`claude-fable-5`, `claude-opus-5`, `claude-sonnet-5`) now classifies in modeltier (it was unknown — Claude 5 sessions ran the universal 0.85 compaction threshold instead of their tier's, on windows of 1M for opus/sonnet-5 and the conservative 200K fallback for fable), `claude-fable-5` gets its 1M context-window entry, and context-window resolution is case-insensitive like modeltier and pricing (an uppercase model id silently disabled threshold-based compaction). Surfaced by a post-merge aggregate audit of the #530–#536 train.
- extras/scion: model aliases move to current-gen gemini — small/medium `gemini-3.5-flash-lite`, large `gemini-3.5-flash`, extra-large `gemini-3.6-flash`, matching core-agent's taskclass tiers. The old large/extra-large mapping `gemini-2.5-pro` couldn't combine search built-ins with function tools (#531), so scion-driven agents could never ground. Fixes [#544](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/544). (#562)
- models/anthropic: tool declarations no longer 400 against Anthropic's strict `input_schema` validation — `schemaToInput` normalizes genai's proto-enum type spellings (`"OBJECT"` → `"object"`, `TYPE_UNSPECIFIED` dropped) recursively across schema positions (`items`/`anyOf`/`properties`) while leaving instance data untouched; and `GenerateContent` now honors the stream flag, yielding exactly one `TurnComplete` response under non-streaming consumers instead of one partial per text fragment. Both hit live on mast's first anthropic-vertex run. Fixes [#532](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/532), [#533](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/533). (#542)
- taskclass/modeltier: the gemini frontier tier default was `gemini-3.5-pro` — a model id that never shipped (3.5 went flash-first), so any frontier-default run failed at the API — and the mid default `gemini-2.5-pro` can't combine search builtins with function tools, so tool-carrying loops could never ground. Frontier moves to `gemini-3.6-flash`, mid to `gemini-3.5-flash`, with modeltier classification, context-window, pricing (allowlist + regen), the TUI picker, and docs moved together. Fixes [#530](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/530), [#531](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/531). (#545)
- models/gemini: the "built-ins require Gemini 3.0+" constraint is now enforced instead of documented — pre-3.0 (or unparseable) models with function tools present skip built-in injection with one operator log line and degrade unGrounded, instead of 400ing every turn; path-qualified ids resolve via their last segment, and the Vertex context-cache seed inherits the guard so cached and uncached turns agree on the tool set. Fixes [#535](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/535). (#546)
- vertexcache: closed the lost-retry race behind the #499 test flake — the transient-cancel test now polls `Init` inside its wait (mirroring the per-turn product contract) instead of issuing one post-blip call that could be swallowed while the Init gate was still closed. Test-only; production always self-healed. Fixes [#534](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/534). (#547)
- e2e/examples: the GKE-troubleshoot e2e now builds the daemon image from the current checkout and side-loads it into kind (override with `DAEMON_IMAGE=<tag>` to smoke a published tag), and the recipe's watcher pin moves to `lookout:v0.11.0` with the daemon pin at the `2.7.0` GA. This closes the skew blind spot that let #383's CSRF guard (PR #431) ship while the e2e stayed green: the e2e pinned a pre-guard `core-agent:2.6.0`, so no CI leg ever ran a current daemon against the shipped watcher — meanwhile in production every per-incident session open failed with `415 unsupported media type`, because lookout ≤ v0.10.0 sends no `Content-Type` on the bodyless `POST /sessions` (fixed in k8s-lookout#139, released as v0.11.0). The e2e's stale `__GCP_PROJECT__` AGENTS.md sed (dead since #323's `${env:VAR}` interpolation) is replaced by a guard that fails if placeholder tokens ever resurface.
- tests: `internal/vertexcache`'s asynchronous assertions no longer flake under full-suite `-race` CI load (#499) — all sixteen `waitFor` sites — plus the one bare `time.After` select with the same exposure — move from a 1s deadline to a shared generous `testWait` (10s). The conditions are goroutine-scheduling hops that resolve in microseconds on an idle scheduler; the deadline only bounds genuinely broken runs, so the passing path is exactly as fast as before. Same treatment as the #462 resume-test flake. Closes [#499](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/499).
- background: `Manager.Close` can no longer hang forever racing a failing `Spawn` (#502, found during #488's adversarial review). `Close` snapshots every handle and blocks on `<-h.done`, but a Spawn that failed during pre-launch resolution (tools/scheduler/model) never launched the goroutine that closes `done` — a Close that snapshotted the reservation inside that window waited on it forever. The three failure paths now roll back through a shared `abortSpawn` that closes `done` alongside the #488 identity-checked unreservation. Deterministic regression test blocks a stub provider mid-resolution, snapshots via a concurrent Close, then releases — hangs pre-fix, returns instantly post-fix.
- agent/attach: the typed operator-event seam is transport-neutral (#506, split from #492 item 8) — `agent.SetOperatorEventEmitter` replaces `SetAttachEmitter` (which baked one transport's name into the deliberately narrow core surface; the events — status-update, usage-update, turn-complete/error, inbox — belong to whoever is listening, attach SSE being merely today's only consumer). `pkg/attach` gains the matching `OperatorEventTarget` capability interface; the broadcaster probes it first and falls back to the deprecated `EmitTarget`, because dropping the fallback would fail SILENTLY — a registrant built against the old method name would just stop emitting typed events. Deprecated wrappers remain on the agent and adapter for one release; a regression test pins the legacy-registrant fallback.
- agent/runner: `agent.Agent` exposes `Model()` — the LLM it was constructed with — and `runner.Run` derives the streaming model from a supplied `Agent` when `RunOptions.Model` is nil (#510), closing the agent/model mismatch hazard the #492 assessment flagged on the old positional variants. Passing both still works (they must match); the bundled CLI's call sites drop the redundant field.
- compose: per-tenant session construction (#505) — `SessionFactoryDeps.Customize`, a hook invoked at the top of every session creation AND lazy resume with the owning caller and a `SessionCustomization` pre-filled from the daemon-wide defaults. Hosts can vary the model (per-turn cost attribution and pricing re-resolve from the layered catalog automatically), the tools, and the toolsets (skills bundles ride as toolsets) without forking `ReproduceAgent`; permissions and instructions were already per-session/per-caller (template-derived sub-gates, `UsersDir` overlays). The pre-filled slices are clones, so a hook's appends can never write through into the shared deps and leak one tenant's tools to another; a hook error aborts construction with the caller identity in the message.
- models: a programmatic provider-construction path (#492 item 7) — `models.New(opts models.ProviderOptions)` with per-provider option structs (`models.GeminiAPI`, `models.GeminiVertex`, `models.AnthropicAPI`, `models.AnthropicVertex`) routes through the same registry as `Resolve`, so embedders no longer fabricate the on-disk `*config.Config` struct just to pick a backend. The structs carry exactly what the routing layer needs — backend plus credentials/deployment; the model ID is deliberately not routing state and goes to `Provider.Model(ctx, name)`, same as with `Resolve`; the per-backend constructors and functional options (`gemini.NewAPIKey`, `anthropic.NewVertex`, …) remain the full-control path, and `Resolve` remains the config-file adapter, unchanged. The interface is sealed so option shapes evolve with the registry.
- attach: the SSE `capabilities` boot frame now reports actual wiredness for adapter-registered sessions. The frame was derived by type-asserting optional interfaces on the registrant, but `attachadapter.Adapter` satisfies all of them unconditionally — so every adapter session (i.e. all of them since #443) advertised `mcp`/`perms_stream`/`specialists`/`interrupt` and all five slash commands regardless of wiring, and remote UIs rendered dead affordances backed by empty payloads, 501s, or 500s (unwired replan). New optional `attach.CapabilityReporter` interface (a `CapabilityReport` of wired features + serviceable slash names) is consulted in preference to interface probing; the adapter implements it from real signals (prompt broker / MCP fn / replanner presence, `HasCompactor`/`HasCheckpointer`, `BackgroundManager() != nil`; interrupt and `btw` are core-agent capabilities). Registrants that only satisfy what they support keep the legacy probing unchanged. Closes [#490](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/490).
- Post-assessment roundup ([#488](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/488)), six smaller fixes in one pass: **attach** — `Server.Close` no longer burns the full `ShutdownTimeout` when streaming clients are attached: the broadcaster pool is closed before `srv.Shutdown` (hanging up `/events`), and a new handlers-level closing latch ends the `/perms/stream` feeds that the pool doesn't own — the remote TUI holds both, so previously every daemon stop with a TUI attached waited out the entire timeout; a request reaching `pool.For` mid-shutdown now gets a clean "shutting down" error instead of the old post-timeout nil-map panic window; **background** — a Spawn whose tool/scheduler/model resolution fails now rolls back its name reservation only if it still owns it, so a `Stop` → terminal-evict → same-name re-spawn landing in the resolution window can no longer have its fresh handle deleted out from under it (orphaned-but-running subagent, name freed for a duplicate); and a finished subagent's terminal alert is suppressed when its name has already been re-spawned, so an old incarnation's "completed" no longer reads as the new one finishing; **compose** — `BuildAttachOptions` keeps an UNSET `$VAR` reference literal (with a warning) instead of silently expanding it to `""` (`"127.0.0.1:$PORT"` with a typo'd var no longer binds an ephemeral port; set-but-empty vars still expand); **dev/tools** — the GKE-troubleshoot e2e's pipeline polls tolerate transient kubectl/curl failures instead of dying under `set -euo pipefail` mid-retry, and `e2e-real-provider` distinguishes "no credentials" from "all legs self-skipped via exit 77" in its summary. Closes [#488](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/488).
- models/anthropic: every streaming response is now `Close()`d (`pkg/models/anthropic/llm.go`). The SDK's `*ssestream.Stream` owns the HTTP response body and no path in `GenerateContent` released it. On a full drain that was a hygiene gap (an EOF-read body returns its connection to the pool anyway), but a consumer stopping mid-iteration — or an accumulate/stream error — left a **live TCP connection established** until the caller's context was cancelled; in-tree callers cancel per turn (bounded), while library consumers with a long-lived context leaked one connection per stop, multiplied by the #461 pause_turn continuation loop (up to 5 streams per call). Each continuation's stream now closes via a deferred `Close` covering early consumer stop, accumulate/stream errors, the continuation hop, and the terminal return. Body-tracking transport tests pin all paths (6 unclosed bodies pre-fix, zero post-fix; the mid-stream connection hold was confirmed at the TCP level during adversarial review). Closes [#487](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/487).
- attach: idle-evicting a session now retires its broadcaster, so a lazily-resumed session no longer binds to the dead agent's. The broadcaster pool keys by session triple and only `DELETE /sessions` ever removed entries, so after evict + resume, `pool.For` handed the fresh Entry the OLD broadcaster: boot snapshots and the `SetAttachEmitter` wiring hit the evicted agent (the resumed agent's typed status/usage/turn events never reached SSE subscribers), and the pump's activity `touch()` refreshed the stale entry — an actively-streaming resumed session looked idle to the sweep and could have its wake loop cancelled mid-work. The registry gains an evict hook (wired by the server to retire the pool entry — quiet SSE clients of an evicted session now get EOF and reconnect into the resume path), and `pool.For` validates Entry identity as a backstop, replacing (and closing) a stale broadcaster for any re-registration path that bypasses the hook. Adversarial review of the initial version confirmed two defects that are also fixed: the `For` backstop is registration-sequence-directed (entries carry a registry-assigned `regSeq`), so a preempted handler holding an older Entry can never unseat the current broadcaster and reinstall a dead-agent one; and shutdown now fences in-flight retirements — `Server.Close` joins the sweep goroutine and `pool.Close` waits on a retiring group covering hook/`For`-swap/DELETE closes, preserving the #424 "nothing reads the eventlog after Close returns" contract. The hook and DELETE path share the identity-checked `pool.Retire`, so a late hook firing can never tear down an already-resumed session's fresh broadcaster. Closes [#486](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/486).
- attach: a transient eventlog `Watch` error (e.g. SQLite "database is locked") no longer permanently freezes a session's live-tail. The pump goroutine died without cleanup: subscribers kept open-but-silent channels (a frozen stream with no EOF to trigger a client reconnect), and the stale `b.cancel` made every future `Subscribe` skip the lazy pump start — one hiccup bricked the stream until every client disconnected at once. A deferred sweep in the pump now makes every exit path converge: all subscribers are detached (channels close, clients reconnect) and the restart latch is cleared, so the next `Subscribe` brings up a fresh pump. The sweep is generation-guarded — a stale pump whose sweep runs late must not hang up a successor pump's subscribers or cancel the successor's context (caught by adversarial review of the initial fix; both interleavings are pinned by deterministic tests). Closes [#485](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/485).
- attach: a `Subscribe` racing `Close` (an `/events` connect racing `DELETE /sessions` or server shutdown — handlers resolve the broadcaster from the pool before subscribing) no longer panics on the nilled subscriber map. Worse than the panic itself: `Subscribe` held the broadcaster mutex with no deferred unlock, so after net/http recovered, the mutex stayed locked forever, wedging every later operation on that broadcaster; and the near-miss interleaving spawned replay goroutines after `Close`'s `wg.Wait` had returned — the exact eventlog use-after-close #424 fenced. `Close` now sets a `closed` flag under the mutex; `Subscribe` registers (and does both `wg.Add`s) inside one deferred-unlock critical section that checks the flag, returning an already-closed channel when it loses the race. 200-iteration `-race` regression test. Closes [#483](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/483).
- attach: the `/events` replay cap (#385) no longer destroys a session's reconnect history when sibling sessions are busy. The cap's floor was computed as `head_seq − 5000` in the eventlog's **global** sequence space — one autoincrement shared by every session — so a busy neighbor pushed a quiet session's rows under the floor and a `since=0` reconnect silently replayed a fraction of the conversation (repro: an 11-event session next to a 6000-row sibling replayed one event). The floor is now the subscribing session's own 5001st-newest seq (indexed `ORDER BY seq DESC LIMIT 1 OFFSET cap` via the new optional `NthNewestSeq` stream extension), so a session at or under the cap always replays in full and one over it replays exactly its newest 5000. Closes [#481](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/481).
- models/anthropic: server-side tools no longer break turns (`pkg/models/anthropic/llm.go`, `stream.go`). With `WithWebSearch` enabled, a long search could end the turn at `pause_turn` — the adapter now resumes it: the paused assistant message is replayed verbatim (SDK `Message.ToParam()`, `server_tool_use`/`web_search_tool_result` blocks intact) and streaming continues, bounded at 4 continuations, with partial text flowing throughout and usage summed across all requests of the turn. Text emitted before a pause is preserved in the terminal response. Server-side tool blocks are deliberately not projected into genai parts (a `FunctionCall` would make the runner dispatch a server-only tool; result payloads are mostly encrypted; the model's own text carries the answer) — the strict replay case is the continuation loop, which is exact; completed turns tolerate their absence in later history per the API contract. Offline SSE-fixture tests cover the two-request continuation, verbatim replay, usage summing, and the loop bound. Closes [#461](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/461).
- deps: `google.golang.org/grpc` bumped v1.81.1 → v1.82.1 for [GO-2026-6061](https://pkg.go.dev/vuln/GO-2026-6061) (reachable via the attach server's HTTP serve path per govulncheck).
- Correctness roundup ([#372](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/372)), eight confirmed low-severity fixes in one pass: autonomous resume now reports real elapsed `Duration` on the cancelled-while-waiting path (was `time.Since(time.Now())` ≈ 0); `Agent.RunWithContents` deletes its per-call throwaway session after the turn instead of leaking one durable `rwc-*` row per call (the AX adapter calls it per-turn); a background subagent NAME is reusable once its handle reaches a terminal status — `Spawn` evicts the finished handle instead of returning `ErrSubagentExists` forever (running holders still error); the LLM recorder snapshots the request eagerly so a wrapper mutating `Config` mid-call can no longer retroactively alter the recording, and encode errors are logged instead of dropped; Anthropic tool-result marshal failures now produce an `is_error=true` block naming the failure instead of a silent empty success; the Anthropic adapter maps generation config it previously dropped — `Temperature`, `TopP`, `TopK`, `StopSequences`, forced tool choice (`ANY`→`any`/single-name `tool`, `NONE`→`none`), and `ThinkingConfig.ThinkingBudget`; a Vertex cache refresh that lands after `MarkEvicted` no longer writes a stale `expiresAt` onto the evicted manager; and the remote pricing-catalog fetch caps the response body at 32 MiB instead of an unbounded `io.ReadAll`. Item 7 of the roundup is tracked separately as [#263](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/263); item 8 split out as [#461](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/461). Closes [#372](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/372).
- internal/vertexcache: cache creation no longer sticky-fails for the daemon's lifetime when the first turn ends before the Create RPC returns (`internal/vertexcache/manager.go`). `Init` ran `Caches.Create` on the first turn's request context, so a completed/interrupted turn killed the RPC with `context.Canceled`, flipped the manager to sticky `stateFailed`, and the session silently paid full input price forever. `doInit`/`doRefresh` now run on a detached (`context.WithoutCancel`) context with their own 2-minute RPC timeout, and cancellation/timeout is classified as transient — the manager resets to its pre-Init state so a later turn retries; only genuine failures (bad model, permission denied) keep the documented sticky behavior. Closes [#370](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/370).
- agent/watchdog: tool-call observations are deduped per turn, fixing repeated-tool-call false positives at ~half the configured threshold (`pkg/agent/watchdog.go`). ADK's streaming aggregator can re-emit the same FunctionCall part across an intermediate aggregate plus the final event — the display path (`runner/events.go`) already deduped this, but the watchdog observer didn't, so every real call counted up to twice. Calls dedup on their ID when present (a re-emitted part keeps its ID; a legitimate parallel call gets a fresh one), falling back to name+args for ID-less providers; the set resets each turn, since cross-turn repetition is exactly the signal the watchdog counts. Closes [#363](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/363).
- models/anthropic: synthesized `tool_use`/`tool_result` IDs are unique per request (`pkg/models/anthropic/convert.go`). ID-less genai parts (parallel calls to the same tool in one turn, or replayed Gemini-origin histories, which frequently omit IDs) all patched in the identical `"call_<name>"`, so Anthropic 400'd the duplicate `tool_use` IDs or mispaired results. A per-request synthesizer now suffixes repeat occurrences (`call_grep`, `call_grep_2`, …) with call-side and result-side counters advancing independently per name, so results keep pairing with their calls by name-occurrence order; the first occurrence keeps the historical bare form, and counters reset per request so persisted sessions re-pair deterministically. Closes [#367](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/367).
- models/anthropic: thinking blocks now round-trip through the genai converters (`stream.go`, `convert.go`), unbreaking tool loops on thinking-default Claude models (`claude-sonnet-5`, `claude-opus-5`, `claude-fable-5`). The adapter previously converted only Text/ToolUse blocks, so the assistant turn replayed before a `tool_result` arrived without its thinking blocks and the API 400'd every second request of a tool loop ("Expected thinking … but found tool_use") — latent until now only because the default model is thinking-off. Thinking blocks map to genai `Thought` parts with the signature in `ThoughtSignature`; `redacted_thinking`'s opaque payload rides behind a marker prefix in the same field and is peeled back on replay. Unsigned thought parts (e.g. Gemini thought summaries after a mid-session provider switch) are dropped on the Anthropic side — the API rejects signature-less thinking blocks and only requires replay of blocks it produced. Closes [#357](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/357).
- eventlog: deleting a session no longer permanently poisons every `Watch`/`Since` consumer. `Service.Delete` now drops the session's `agent_eventlog` overlay rows alongside ADK's rows (previously they were orphaned, and their `loadEvent` failed with "session not found" forever), and `Watch` now advances its cursor past a row that fails to hydrate — surfacing it once instead of re-querying and re-erroring it every poll interval. Closes [#354](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/354).
- eventlog: `SessionLock` now detects a stolen lease and signals it instead of writing on regardless (split-brain). If the heartbeat's conditional UPDATE matches zero rows — another process reclaimed the lease after a `>staleAfter` stall — the lock closes the new `SessionLock.Lost()` channel and stops heartbeating, so a run loop can select on it and abort. Each heartbeat is also bounded by a timeout, so a wedged database can no longer hang the heartbeat goroutine (or `Release`, which waits on it) indefinitely. Closes [#358](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/358).
- eventlog: `Handle.Close` now shuts down ADK's connection pool as well as the overlay's. `Open` opens two GORM pools against the same database (ADK's inside `NewSessionService` plus our overlay), but `Close` previously closed only the overlay — leaking ADK's connections and file handles on every Open/Close cycle (tests, multi-daemon session churn). Closes [#360](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/360).
- eventlog: `Since`/`Watch` no longer replay in O(N²). Hydrating overlay rows previously re-fetched and linearly scanned the entire session for every row, so replaying an N-event session issued N full-session loads. A single per-pass index now loads each distinct session at most once and hydrates each row via a map lookup — linear replay, and `Watch` no longer pays a full session load per newly-arrived event. Closes [#361](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/361).
- Usage: context-window table corrected for current-gen Claude models. `ContextWindowSizeFor` mapped every `claude-opus-4*` / `claude-sonnet-4*` to 200K unless a `-1m` suffix was present, but Opus 4.6/4.7/4.8 and Sonnet 4.6 now ship a 1M window with no suffix (as do Opus 5 / Sonnet 5). On the default `claude-opus-4-7` this made the `0.85 × window` compaction threshold trip at ~17% of the real window — compacting sessions that had used a fraction of their available context. The table now returns 1M for those models; earlier 4.x (Opus 4.0/4.1/4.5, Sonnet 4.0/4.5) keep the 200K base + `-1m` opt-in, Haiku 4.5 stays 200K, and unknown `claude-*` still defaults conservatively to 200K. Closes [#369](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/369).
- Usage: unknown-model turns no longer masquerade as free ($0). `PriceFor` / `PriceForWithSource` discarded the pricing catalog's `found` flag, so a model with no rate resolved to a zero `Pricing` that was indistinguishable from a genuinely free model — the resulting $0 flowed silently into `usage.Tracker` totals and the `core_agent.session.cost_usd` metric. `Pricing` now carries an `Unpriced` flag (set from `!found`) that threads into `Turn.Unpriced` and is aggregated as `Totals.UnpricedTurns` by both `Totals()` and `TotalsByModel()`; the cost metric gains a `priced` attribute so dashboards can tell an exact total from a lower bound. Consumers rendering cost should show "$—" for unpriced turns instead of "$0.00". Closes [#368](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/368).
- Cost ceiling: enforce the per-turn cap against the fully-settled turn. `maybeEnforceCostCeiling` ran only in the post-turn cleanup hook, but in harness-driven deployments the harness calls `tracker.Append` for the turn's main-model cost *after* that hook — so the per-turn delta saw only in-turn internal appends (subtasks, summarizer) and missed the main-model spend entirely, and a single runaway turn (the #144 read-file loop the cap was built for) could never trip it. Enforcement now also runs at the top of the next `Run`, before the turn-start snapshot is retaken, so the prior turn's true cost (now committed to the tracker) is measured; a runaway turn trips the cap by the start of the following turn, which `preflightCostCeiling` then refuses. Closes [#362](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/362).
- Background subagents: close the Stop-during-spawn race that stranded a "stopped" agent that kept running. `Spawn` registered the handle in `m.agents` (making it addressable by `Stop`) before wiring `handle.cancel`, which was set only after the slow, network-capable tool/scheduler/model resolution. A `Stop` arriving in that window found `cancel == nil`, marked the handle `Stopped`, and returned — yet the goroutine still launched and ran the full autonomous loop, burning budget while reporting "stopped". The goroutine's cancellable context and `handle.cancel` are now created and registered up front, before the handle becomes visible, and every pre-launch error path releases it; a `Stop` in the resolution window now cancels the context so `RunAutonomous` exits before its first turn. Closes [#366](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/366).
- Autonomous driver: write the final checkpoint on a detached context. The autonomous/resume loops emit a final checkpoint on every exit path, including context cancellation — but \`emitFinalCheckpoint\` passed the loop's \`ctx\` straight to \`emitCheckpoint\`, so on the cancellation/shutdown path the DB write failed against the already-dead context and the checkpoint was silently lost, precisely when crash-resume needs it most. The final checkpoint now writes through a context detached from cancellation (\`context.WithoutCancel\` + a bounded \`finalCheckpointWriteTimeout\`), so it lands on shutdown while a wedged session service still can't hang process exit. Closes [#365](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/365).
- Subagents: isolate each invocation in its own derived session row. \`deriveSubagentSessionID\` was deterministic (\`<parent>:sub:<branch>\`) and computed once at tool construction, so every call to a given subagent shared one row. Because \`DefaultInstruction\` urges parallel tool calls, two concurrent invocations of the same subagent interleaved each other's in-flight history and raced ADK's optimistic-concurrency check, while sequential invocations silently accumulated history across supposedly independent requests. The derived ID now carries an invocation-unique component (ADK's \`FunctionCallID\`, or a fresh UUID when absent) computed per call. The shared \`<parent>:sub:<branch>\` prefix and branch tag are preserved so audit queries still find every invocation; the name-addressed subtask and background-spawn paths intentionally keep their deterministic rows. Closes [#364](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/364).
- Agent turn lifecycle: stop leaking the per-turn context and fix `Interrupt` becoming a no-op. `Run` wrapped each turn in a `context.WithCancel` but only ever called `cancel()` from `Interrupt` — an uninterrupted turn returned without cancelling, leaking a live cancellable child of the process-lifetime parent every turn (a classic `lostcancel`; thousands accrue in a long-lived daemon). The cleanup path now always calls `cancel()` once the event stream has fully drained. Separately, the in-flight cancel was cleared with a `reflect.Value.Pointer()` identity check, but every `context.WithCancel` cancel shares the same code pointer, so the guard was always true — a late-firing older turn's cleanup could silently clobber a newer turn's registered cancel, making `Interrupt` a no-op. Cancel registrations now carry a monotonic generation token and cleanup only clears when the generation still matches. Closes [#359](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/359).
- Summarizer (compaction/checkpoint): bound input and stop silently swallowing failures. `runSummarizer` loaded the full unsliced session history on every call, so each successive compaction's LLM input was strictly larger than the conversation that triggered it (it re-fed every prior summary + all pre-boundary turns) and long sessions eventually blew past the summarizer's own context window — wedging at the wall while every turn re-flagged pending and paid for a doomed call. It now bounds input to the current window (from the latest boundary forward via the new `summarizerHistory`), which still carries the prior summary for continuity. Failures are no longer discarded: auto-compaction failures are logged and trigger exponential turn-based backoff (via `compactionBackoffTurns`) instead of retrying every turn, and the auto-checkpoint failure path now logs instead of writing to a `devNull` sink. Closes [#356](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/356).
- Compact/Checkpoint: guard against a mid-turn boundary write. `Agent.Compact` / `Agent.Checkpoint` (reachable out-of-band via `AttachCompact` and the TUI `/compact` + `/done` slashes) appended a boundary event directly to the parent session row with no in-flight-turn check. Firing mid-turn raced the runner's own `AppendEvent` (ADK optimistic-concurrency failure against its stale snapshot) and could wedge a boundary between a persisted `functionCall` and its `functionResponse`, making the history-slicing path emit an orphaned `functionResponse` that Gemini rejects. Both now return the new `ErrTurnInFlight` sentinel while a turn is running; the automatic threshold-driven path (`runPendingCompaction` / `runPendingCheckpoint`) runs before the turn's cancel is registered and is unaffected. Closes [#355](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/355).
- Autonomous driver: fix double-counting of Gemini's cumulative per-chunk `UsageMetadata`. `runOneTurn` accumulated tokens and called `tracker.AppendUsage` on *every* event carrying `UsageMetadata`, but Gemini reports usage cumulatively across streaming chunks within a model turn. This inflated `WithMaxTokens`/`WithMaxCost` budgets (tripping early), background subagents' contribution to the parent `/stats`, and the recorded turn count (one Append per chunk). The driver now routes usage through `usage.TurnTap` — commit exactly once per `TurnComplete` — matching `subtask.go`. Closes [#353](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/353).
- Attach: fix a data race + panic in the SSE broadcaster's dual-source send path that could crash the whole daemon. `replayThenTail` (and `deliverBootFrames`) called `send`/`sendTyped` without holding `b.mu`, so a slow SSE consumer hitting a full buffer during replay ran `detachLocked` (`delete(b.subs)` + `close(sub.ch)`) concurrently with the `pump` goroutine's locked iterate-and-send over the same map/channel — producing "send on closed channel" or "concurrent map writes" and taking down every session on the daemon. Both delivery sources now funnel through `b.mu`; added a regression test that drives the concurrent dual-source send under `-race`. Closes [#377](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/377).
- Attach: `Broadcaster.Close` is now a quiescence barrier — it waits for the pump and replay goroutines to exit before returning, so `Server.Close` can no longer hand back to a caller that closes the eventlog while a `Stream.Watch`/`Since` read is still in flight (a use-after-close on the handle; in tests it surfaced as a flaky `TempDir RemoveAll: directory not empty` on the SQLite `-wal`/`-shm` sidecar files). Close cancels the pump, latches a new `closing` channel to wake post-replay tail-waiters independent of their request contexts, then `wg.Wait()`s the tracked goroutines. Added a deterministic regression test. Closes [#423](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/423).
- `agentenv.Resolver` no longer races under multi-session load. `seenRefs` (the interpolation-tracking set feeding drift reports) was mutated without a lock, but the resolver's `InterpolateFunc` is shared across sessions — concurrent `POST /sessions` → `reproduceAgent` → `Interpolate` wrote the map from several goroutines at once (a guaranteed `-race` failure, and a "concurrent map writes" panic under load). `seenRefs` is now guarded by a mutex and `ReportDrift` snapshots it under the lock. Added a `-race` regression test. Closes [#371](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/371).
- Config persistence is now safe for its own schema (`pkg/config`). `Save` preserves unknown top-level keys on round-trip (an older binary's `Load → mutate → Save` no longer drops sections a newer binary wrote), retains the existing file mode instead of hardcoding `0644` (new files are created `0600` since the schema can hold `api_key`), and stops materializing substrate defaults into a partial config (so a future default bump — e.g. the default model — still reaches operators who never pinned it). `Load` now warns on unknown top-level keys so a security-relevant typo (`permisions`/`denny`) surfaces instead of being silently ignored. Closes [#387](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/387).
- CLI flag drift fixed in `cmd/core-agent` (`--model`, `--compaction-threshold`, `--ask`). The startup summary and task-class message told operators to "override individual knobs with `--model` / `--compaction-threshold`", but neither long flag was registered — `--model` was a silent no-op and `--compaction-threshold` was unrecognized. Both now exist: `--model` is a long-form alias of `-m`, and `--compaction-threshold` (a `(0,1)` fraction, validated) overrides the config-file value and any task profile. Separately, `--ask` defaulted to `"off"`, which made the task-profile ask-mode branch dead code (`ask == ""` was never true); it now defaults to the `""` sentinel so a task profile's ask mode can fill in when the operator leaves `--ask` unset, and `--task` no longer clobbers a model pinned in the config file (it only fills the model when neither `--model` nor a config-file value is set). Closes [#395](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/395).

#### Documentation
- attach: the JSON REST response shapes (`GET /sessions`, `POST /sessions`, `GET /whoami`) are now pinned by `rest-*-v1` conformance fixtures + tests — including a live-handler envelope check and a byte-exact `{"sessions":[]}`-never-`null` pin — closing the gap that let a downstream mock drift to snake_case field names undetected; `attach-http.md`'s own `session_id` misdocumentation corrected to `sessionID`. Fixes [#536](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/536). (#549)
- Refresh the three project-identity docs (`AGENTS.md`, `docs/DESIGN.md`, `docs/north-star.md`) that had drifted behind the codebase: `AGENTS.md`'s layout section now reflects the `pkg/` + `internal/` + `cmd/` tree (was still showing the pre-reorg top-level packages) and no longer claims "no built-in tools, no TUI"; `DESIGN.md`'s non-goals and "deliberately out of scope" section now carry per-item Status lines for the features that have since shipped (built-in tools, TUI, durable sessions, subagents, Anthropic cost/caching) while preserving the original v1 rationale; `north-star.md` gains a status banner noting v2.7.0 GA shipped and the dated critical-path list is now historical.
- Add `docs/cleanup-execution-plan.md` — the parallel-agent execution plan for the four `Cleanup: *` milestones from the 2026-07-25 code-review sweep (conflict-domain lanes, Auto/Assisted/Human-led classification, wave sequencing, and automode guardrails).
- Add `docs/agent-package-split-design.md` — the Wave 3 design for decomposing the `pkg/agent` god package into a stable core plus focused sibling packages (`agent/autonomous`, `agent/background`, `attachadapter`) behind a narrow seam, with a clean-break attach adapter, before the v2 surface freezes. Design-doc-first; tracks [#388](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/388).
- Backfill the `docs/README.md` design-doc registry so all 66 design/reference docs are discoverable per the `AGENTS.md` registration mandate (was registering ~35), regrouped into themed sections; fixed the stale "Hugo" site references to Astro Starlight and pointed the embedded-TUI entry at the current `embedded-tui-design-v2.md` (marking `embedded-tui-design.md` superseded). Closes [#392](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/392).

#### Security
- attach: lazy session resume no longer runs before authorization and the cost rate limiter. A `Lookup` miss on any session-scoped endpoint reconstructed the full session — agent construction, instruction load, unbounded eventlog replay into the tracker, wake-loop spawn — before the caller's ACL check and before the #463 limiter, so a caller destined for a 404 or 429 forced exactly the work those checks exist to bound (and gained a mild session-existence/cost oracle). Now the cost limiter fires before entry lookup on the six cost-limited routes (the five slash ops + `pricing/refresh`; an over-limit caller gets 429 before the lookup outcome is computed), and with ACL enforcement on, a resume is pre-authorized against the persisted ACL row — evaluated per-caller outside the resume singleflight so one caller's deny can never become another's shared result; a deny returns the standard indistinguishable 404. In-memory lookups and the post-lookup per-action authorize are unchanged. Closes [#484](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/484).
- compose: concurrent "allow always" / `/allow` / `/deny` persists no longer lose grants. Every `.agents/config.json` helper (`ConfigGrantStore.Persist`, the `Append*` and `Persist*` functions) was an unguarded `config.Load → mutate → config.Save` read-modify-write over one shared file — and one store instance is shared by every per-session sub-gate — so racing writers overwrote each other's snapshots (repro: 32 concurrent appends left 2–3 grants on disk). Losing a **deny** entry is fail-open. A package-level mutex now serializes all in-process config read-modify-writes; `config.Save`'s atomic rename is unchanged. Closes [#482](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/482).
- Attach/security roundup ([#385](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/385)), six lower-severity hardenings in one pass: `fetch_url` strips operator-injected headers (e.g. `X-Api-Key` from `url_scope.headers`) on every redirect hop and re-applies only the bundle matching the redirect target, so credentials no longer leak across allowlisted hosts (Go only strips `Authorization`/`Cookie` itself); plan-first gating no longer exempts `fetch_url` — network egress before a plan is recorded was an exfiltration channel, so under `require_plan_artifact` it now denies until `record_plan` runs (the read-only `skill` namespace stays exempt, with the rationale documented); the `acceptEdits` blast radius is documented loudly in code and on the site — it auto-allows out-of-scope writes to any path (`~/.bashrc`, `~/.ssh/authorized_keys`) and is a sandbox-only mode; `GET /whoami`'s `source` now reports only what the server itself verified (bearer-table hit, listener-verified mTLS, validated `ProxyHeader` assertion) instead of echoing spoofable request headers — `"iap"` is reserved and never emitted until real gateway validation exists; the `/events` `?since=0` replay is capped at the most recent 5000 events per connection (tail kept, truncation logged) closing a cheap authenticated large-read, with an indexed `LatestSeq` query backing the clamp; and the SSE boot-frame ordering race is fixed — the `capabilities` frame is written to a new subscriber before it is published to the fan-out set, so a typed frame can never precede it (200-iteration race test). Item 4 of the roundup (protocol-version negotiation) had already shipped as [#389](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/389); per-caller rate limiting split out as [#463](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/463). Closes [#385](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/385).
- `fetch_url`: proxying is explicit-config only (`pkg/tools/fetch.go`, `pkg/config`). The transport no longer honors ambient `HTTP_PROXY`/`HTTPS_PROXY` env vars by default — when a proxy is in the path, the guarded `DialContext` validates/pins the proxy address while the *target's* name resolution happens at the proxy, outside the SSRF guard, so a poisoned or forgotten proxy env var could pull hostname targets out of the private/metadata screening. The new `url_scope.proxy` field makes the choice explicit (`"env"` for the standard env vars, or a fixed `http(s)`/`socks5` URL, validated at config load); either non-empty mode is documented as delegating hostname-target SSRF policy to the proxy, and literal-IP screening stays local on the initial URL and every redirect hop. See the ⚠️ Breaking changes entry for the upgrade note. Closes [#429](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/429).
- `fetch_url`: the SSRF guard's IP classification now covers the special-purpose ranges adjacent to the original #375 policy set (`pkg/tools/fetch.go`). Hard-blocked (metadata tier, `url_scope.allow_metadata_endpoints` is the only opt-out): `192.0.0.0/24` — IETF protocol assignments, including the `192.0.0.192` metadata endpoint some cloud environments use. Blocked-unless-exact-host-allowlisted (private tier, same as RFC1918): `0.0.0.0/8` and the unspecified addresses `0.0.0.0`/`::` (connecting to `0.0.0.0` reaches loopback on Linux), the deprecated IPv4-compatible embedding `::/96`, the NAT64 prefixes `64:ff9b::/96` + `64:ff9b:1::/48` (an IPv6 literal can embed a private IPv4), benchmarking `198.18.0.0/15`, limited broadcast, and IPv4/IPv6 multicast. Closes [#428](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/428).
- `fetch_url` now ships an SSRF guard. Link-local/cloud-metadata addresses (`169.254.0.0/16` including `169.254.169.254`, `fe80::/10`, AWS IMDSv6 `fd00:ec2::254`) are hard-blocked in every permission mode regardless of the allowlist — the only opt-out is the new `url_scope.allow_metadata_endpoints` config flag (default false). Loopback/RFC1918/CGNAT/IPv6-ULA destinations are blocked unless the request host is named by an exact (non-wildcard) `url_scope.allow` entry, so the broad wildcard entries the tool description encourages for internal services no longer reach private ranges implicitly. The DNS-rebinding TOCTOU between the allowlist check and the dial is closed by pinning: the transport's `DialContext` resolves the host once, validates every returned IP (one bad IP rejects the set), and dials only a vetted IP while TLS SNI/Host keep the original hostname — re-run on every redirect hop. Also threads the caller's turn context into the request (was `context.Background()`), so `/interrupt` aborts an in-flight fetch. Closes [#375](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/375).
- Permissions: documented the built-in bash denylist explicitly as best-effort defense-in-depth, not a security boundary (`pkg/permissions/denylist.go`, `gate.go`, and the site permissions/quickstart pages). The gate docs previously called it "non-overridable" without acknowledging that a small regex set plus a hard-coded `rm -rf` target list is trivially evadable (quoting, `$IFS`/variable indirection, staging via `curl … -o /tmp/x; sh /tmp/x`, and uncovered targets like `rm -rf /etc`) — and that it is the only bash protection left in yolo mode or after a session/verb grant. The docs now spell out the known bypass classes and recommend allowlist-based execution (the safecmd-guarded prefix allow rules) as the hardened posture. No behavior change. Closes [#381](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/381).
- Permissions: `DecisionAllowSessionTool` grants for namespaced toolsets are now keyed per underlying tool instead of per whole namespace (`pkg/permissions/gate.go`, `pkg/tools/gate.go`). Every MCP tool was gated under the single `"mcp"` namespace, so approving "allow every call to this tool" for a benign `filesystem_read_file` silently granted blanket, un-prompted session approval to every MCP tool from every server (including mutating ones); the `"skill"` namespace had the same collision. A new `Gate.CheckToolCall(namespace, tool, key)` keys the grant as `"<namespace>/<tool>"`, matching the specific tool the user saw named in the prompt; policy allow/deny matching still uses the namespace (`mcp:<tool>` / `skill:<tool>` grammar unchanged). Any pre-existing in-session blanket `"mcp"` grant matches nothing under the new keying (safer). Closes [#379](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/379).
- Permissions: a per-tool session grant no longer silently drops the path boundary for file tools (`pkg/permissions/gate.go`). `CheckFileRead`/`CheckFileWrite` returned early on `sessionToolAllowed` before consulting the path scope, so choosing "session-tool" for `read_file`/`write_file` granted reads/writes to any path on the filesystem for the session, not just in-scope paths. The scope check now runs first: a per-tool session grant suppresses the mode prompt for in-scope operations, but out-of-scope paths still escalate via the path-scope prompt (or are denied where prompting is impossible). Closes [#380](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/380).
- Permissions/hooks: closed a self-escalation + persistence path through the `.agents/` control plane (`pkg/permissions/gate.go`, `pkg/tools/file.go`, `pkg/hooks/dispatcher.go`). Writes to the privilege-bearing control-plane files — the agent config (`.agents/config.json`) and MCP config (`.agents/mcp.json`), in both the project and home `.agents/` trees — now require a new elevated interactive approval that is never satisfied by `yolo`/`acceptEdits` mode, session/verb/tool grants, allowlist entries, or built-in bundles; with no interactive prompter available the write is denied. Classification runs on the symlink-resolved path, so a symlink laundering the target is still caught. Instruction-bearing files (`AGENTS.md`, skills content) stay normally writable. Separately, hook commands — previously run via `/bin/sh -c` with no gate, so an injected `hooks` entry in a model-written config could execute arbitrary shell — are now routed through the permission gate as a bash-kind check (denylist + mode + deny patterns) before execution; a dispatcher with no gate fails closed and runs nothing. Closes [#378](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/378).
- Permissions: file-tool path scope now resolves symlinks before every access check, closing a traversal escape (`pkg/tools/file.go`, `pkg/permissions/scope.go`). The gate previously checked the lexical path while `os.ReadFile`/`atomicWrite`/`os.Remove` followed symlinks, so a symlink inside the project root (pre-planted in an untrusted repo, or created via an approved/yolo `ln -s`) pointing at `/etc`, `~/.ssh`, or `~/.aws/credentials` let the model read or overwrite arbitrary targets while the gate saw an in-scope path — `read_many_files` with explicit paths being the cleanest read-exfil vector. `absolutize` and `PathScope.AccessFor` now resolve via `filepath.EvalSymlinks` (deepest-existing-ancestor for not-yet-created write targets) with no opt-out; a path whose resolution escapes scope is treated exactly like any out-of-scope path (prompts in ask mode, denied where prompting is impossible), and resolution errors other than not-exist fail closed. Scope roots that themselves sit behind a symlink are matched in both spellings. Closes [#374](https://github.com/go-steer/core-agent/issues/374).
- Permissions: prefix-matched (`<verb> *`) bash allowlist rules — including the default `read_only` built-in bundle — no longer auto-allow chained, piped, redirected, or expansion-bearing commands.
